tbmm-2018-10-17sit10spe0451par0200	Mr. President, members of parliament, I have made a pledge on behalf of the National Party of Movement to address the second part of the main Law Proposal (2/860). Of Highway 2918, articles on the basis of the amendment to the Traffic Law, Article 23 (P.S.), the obligation to consider the speed and distance of vehicles in the crossings of pedestrians in the 68th article of the law, where there is no light or authority. As you know, it is important for communities to prioritize pedestrians in traffic and ensure the safety of pedestrians in traffic to show the level of civilization. In our country, we have citizens who have been injured or killed on foot in car accidents as many as our citizens have been injured or killed. These losses hurt each of us, and the statistical data that emerges every year is what we call traffic accidents as traffic terrorism. Increased social education in preventing traffic accidents is considered the most important measure. However, increasing the efficiency of traffic controls and deterrence of criminal sanctions are also a key measure. The changes in this scope are appropriate. The change in Article 23 and the first right of passage to pedestrians in Article 25 have also been justified. In the traffic accidents involving pedestrians, it is expressed in many judicial decisions in which pedestrians can also cause accidents and therefore have legal responsibilities. If judgment is flawed, pedestrians face compensation obligations in the amount of defects they are burdened with. With this bell lifted, we believe that there will be a serious decrease in compensation cases for pedestrian traffic accidents, or serious decreases in the level of defects that have been imposed on pedestrians. Again, with the change in the 25th article, the first right of access to pedestrians in traffic, we expect the injuries of pedestrians within the city and the decrease of accidents resulting in their deaths. An increase in punishment in violation of this clause would be a useful change in deterrentism. It is important that higher fines are foreseen for drivers who use mobile phones in the 24th article of the proposal. Research has been known by public opinion that it is more dangerous for drivers to use a mobile phone in traffic than to drive a DUI. When we look past vehicles that are traveling in traffic every day and are dangerously changing lanes, doing meaningless maneuvers, we see that the driver has a cell phone in his hand. Especially today, when smartphones are so common, the danger has increased considerably more. It is also important that traffic police who are responsible for the inspection do so without exception. In Article 26 of the proposal, the application of administrative fines to car owners who allow to be driven with vehicles other than the purpose shown in the motor vehicle registration document and are banned from traffic for 15 days. The following jokes indicate that it is forbidden to carry passengers within the municipality's borders except in the field of activity or in the matter mentioned in the permit. Those who violate this rule will be fined with a high fine of 15 to 60 days from traffic. In general, it is clear that with these changes that we see to be accurate, the goal is to make the activities of passenger transport within the municipality more orderly. Indeed, it has been a good thing that punishments are deterrents in passenger transport activities, especially in terms of non-lawful activities in staff and student transport. However, we also feel that some problems stemming from the practice should be addressed. While the clause includes regulation of passenger transport within municipal borders, we find that there is no mention of passenger transport under the Highway Transport Act and regulations. Dear lawmakers, the complexity of implementing the legislation on passenger transport in Turkey must be resolved immediately. It must be ensured that the UKOME (UKOME) and the transport regulations that enforce the road transport laws, which are authorized within the city's municipal borders, and that the areas of the transport area, which have the authority and responsibility, and that they act in harmony with the implementation. There are serious complications in implementing legislation in the current situation, and the wrong practices produce damages and victims that cannot be compensated for in the passenger transport sector. The laws on road transport in Turkey must ensure that the norm and standard union is formed. In addition, implementors must have sufficient information equipment for legislation. For example, when traffic police stop a vehicle carrying passengers in their inspections, they hesitate to determine which laws or rules or which UKOME decision to conduct the inspection. Traffic police can be personally responsible for their administrative fines and traffic bans. When wrong decisions are made, wrongful practices are brought to justice, the situation becomes even more insurmountable by the verdicts made by the judiciary. In Turkey, where land transport is vital, it is an urgent need to make these activities more regular in both legislation and practice. In regard to Article 27 of the proposal, we think it would be a fairer solution if the age predicted for the transition of expert gendarme staff to petty officer was determined not as 35 but as 45 years old. If police officers under the general law can apply for the deputy commissioner until they are 45 years old, they should also be able to apply for a transfer to petty officer until they are 45 years old. In Article 28 of the law proposal we have discussed, it has been suggested that the following of crimes involving Anti-Terror Law can be awarded a reward of money to those who help to find or capture evidence or help to capture the perpetrators or reveal their whereabouts or IDs. It is also arranged that the procedure and the basis for giving the award and determining the amount of the reward will be determined by the regulations issued by the Ministry of Interior. According to this, the reward is not absolute, the procedure and the fundamentals will be determined by regulations, and the award will be given or not given in accordance with the consent of the authority. A reward for terrorist crimes or criminal reports must be set on concrete and precise criteria in the regulation the Ministry of Interior will draft to determine the procedure and the basis. It is a common responsibility for all of us to prevent these acts of treason against those who have paid a great price in the fight against terrorism, but who, for one moment, did not compromise their decision, and who set out to expose the Turkish nation and destroy the state of the Republic of Turkey. God forbid, when any attack occurs, it is necessary to ensure that the reward system in every country in the world, which is being implemented in order to ensure that those responsible will be held accountable in front of the law, will also have to be implemented in some way in our country. In this regard, the change is true and appropriate. Again, Article 33 of the proposal envisions expanding the scope of the seizure bonus as part of the fight against trafficking. As we have stated, the high motivation of the anti-trafficking elements is significant in terms of the elimination of legal and immoral proposals to those elements. The proposed arrangement welcomes the award to include personnel involved in all phases of the investigation and operation process, but not personally and in fact in the seizure process. It is an important change in Article 34 of the proposal to include lighter fluid and similar drugs. Because our youth is so different, it's poisoned with drugs that we don't know. Some of these can be obtained in exchange for serious money, but some are available in the street very easily and very cheaply, such as lighter fluid. Inclusion of other substances of all derivatives other than lighter fluids will contribute to increasing the activity in the fight against drugs, including the term "plague gas" and so on. Under Article 32 of the proposed law, foreigners are expected to receive a foreign identity number.
